GEO / Learning experiments

GEO 学习实验记录

持续记录学习过程中的提问、实验、数据与阶段性认识。

阶段一 · AI 会推荐谁，又引用了谁？

ChatGPT Search 与 Grok 的推荐对象、引用来源和回答差异观察。这是当前阶段的学习成果，后续实验将继续补充到本仓库。

阶段一 · 公开记录

个人上下文中与实验无关的片段已省略，并保留编辑标记；公开来源链接已去除跟踪参数。推荐对象、引用归属与统计保持一致。私人会话、截图及完整本地档案不公开。

下载阶段一公开数据 · 公开范围说明 · 学习记录与阶段索引

先看结论

四个主要发现

  • 创作者题中，两平台选出的推荐对象差异最大。合并各自两轮名单后，共同对象仅 2 / 20，跨平台重合度为 10.0%。
  • 本批 ChatGPT 的推荐更常伴随对象自有内容引用。ChatGPT 为 47/48（97.9%），Grok 为 39/76（51.3%）。被推荐、被提及和被引用可以分开出现。
  • 没有一个平台在三类问题上都更稳定。本批实证研究题中 Grok 两轮更接近；学习资源题和创作者题则没有同样明显的差距。
  • 这些差异不能说明某个品牌的 GEO 优化做得好或差。本次衡量的是特定问题与条件下的可见性，未验证优化干预带来的效果，也不代表点击、购买或转化。
阅读口径

当前 12 / 12 份主回答完整，共 142 条已编码“主体×回答”观察。实际提交 22 次、采集 37 次；另有 10 次未纳入主样本的提问，完整保留且不并入统计。

每题每平台只有两次重复，测试跨日、思考强度存在变化，并观察到账户记忆影响。实验没有优化前基线、干预或对照组，因此不能作因果判断或平台固有优劣排名。

两平台选出的推荐对象，差异有多大？

  • 创作者题的跨平台分歧最明显。不同平台选出的资源并不相同，下方三个问题的重合度分别展示这种差异。
  • 共同推荐名单也不同。ChatGPT 与 Grok 在 Q3 各自形成了一组两轮都出现的对象；这只是本次观察，不能推断为长期平台偏好。
下方先合并每个平台的两轮推荐，再比较两个集合：跨平台重合度 = 共同对象 ÷ 两平台并集。每组分别有 2 份完整回答；这与下一节逐平台的“两次回答重合度”使用不同的集合，不能混为一项指标。

Q3 · 创作者题的共同推荐名单

ChatGPT Search · 两轮都推荐

Ahrefs、Aleyda Solis (LearningAIsearch.com / SEOFOMO)、Kevin Indig (Growth Memo)、Search Engine Land、Seer Interactive

Grok · 两轮都推荐

HeyCatch、Indie Hackers、Indietool、Solo Business Hub

Q1 的两轮推荐数分别为 ChatGPT Search 10 / 9 个；Grok 24 / 25 个。学习资源题中 Grok 名单更长；名单长度不能直接表示回答质量或稳定性。Q3 的共同推荐名单只描述本次两轮，不能推断为平台长期偏好。

同一问题，两次回答有多相似？

  • 本批没有一个平台在所有问题上都更稳定。实证研究题中 Grok 两轮更接近，另外两题的差距较小。
  • 这些数值只描述回答的一致程度。日期、思考强度和账号上下文没有完全控制，不能据此评价平台固有优劣。
推荐对象重合度（Jaccard）= 共同推荐数 ÷ 至少出现一次的推荐对象数；该值不衡量准确性或效果。下方同时列出每组的条件差异与未知字段。

被引用、被提及、被推荐，可以分开出现

  • 本批 ChatGPT 的推荐更常伴随对象自有内容引用。下方用相同定义、各自的推荐记录作分母比较。
  • 引用、提及和推荐可以分开出现。内容被用作依据，不一定带来品牌曝光；对象被推荐，也可能由第三方内容支撑。
C · Citation
对象自己发布或署名的内容被回答引用。
M · Mention
回答正文明确点名对象。
R · Recommendation
回答主动建议使用、阅读或关注对象。
C/M/R 分别判断，不构成严格的三级漏斗；同一对象可以同时满足其中多项。未见自有引用不代表回答没有依据。

推荐记录中，有多少同时获得自有内容引用？

ChatGPT Search

97.9%

47 / 48 条推荐同时引用对象自有或署名内容

另有 1 条推荐未观察到这类引用；0 条尚未判定。

Grok

51.3%

39 / 76 条推荐同时引用对象自有或署名内容

另有 37 条推荐未观察到这类引用；0 条尚未判定。
分母是本批已编码的推荐记录，不是所有品牌，也不是品牌被推荐的概率。未见自有引用不等于回答没有依据。

C/M/R 组合分布

共 142 条“主体×回答”记录；同一对象在两份回答中出现算两条。条形宽度按各平台观察数归一，文字给出绝对数量；未列字母均为“否”。对象包括品牌、机构、创作者和研究，不全是产品。
C＋M＋RM＋R，无自有 CC＋M，无 R仅 C仅 M
ChatGPT Search57 条主体×回答观察
475

C＋M＋R 47；M＋R，无自有 C 1；C＋M，无 R 2；仅 C 5；仅 M 2

Grok85 条主体×回答观察
39379

C＋M＋R 39；M＋R，无自有 C 37；C＋M，无 R 0；仅 C 0；仅 M 9

编码范围限制：只统计已编码的主要推荐与实质讨论对象，没有穷尽所有来源出版者。Grok 的来源专属对象未全量编码，不能把“仅 C＝0”解释为它不会只引用而不提及。本批问题本身要求推荐，高推荐占比也不能代表市场平均水平。

这些结果不能判断某个品牌的 GEO 优化做得好或差

可以比较的是：本组问题、日期、账号与模式下，对象获得了多少引用、提及和推荐。这不是优化效果、内容质量或平台固有优劣的排名，也不代表点击、购买或转化。

本次没有预先固定待评品牌名单，没有实施优化干预，也没有优化前基线或对照组；每题每平台只有两次重复。测试跨日、思考强度变化，且观察到账户记忆影响。未出现的对象不能直接判为“GEO 做得差”。

若要评估优化效果，需要固定品牌、问题与条件，加入优化前基线、对照和更多重复，分别追踪 C/M/R 的变化。多个教程转述同一篇 GEO 论文也不等于多份独立实证；本报告未独立核验外部研究和效果主张。

主动推荐对象出现频次

  • 同一对象的可见性随问题和平台改变。下方按问题并列展示两平台的推荐次数，用于定位对象在哪类回答中出现。
每个对象每条回答最多计 1 次，分母是该组完整回答数；所有对象默认展开。完整 C/M/R 判定和证据保留在原始记录。

以下为方法与完整证据

全部提问日期：2026-10-29 / 2026-10-30 · 时区：Asia/Shanghai

ChatGPT Search：GPT-6 · 思考强度 Extra High / Medium · 网页搜索 · 2026-10-29 / 2026-10-30
Grok：具体模型版本未披露 · 思考强度 未披露 · Expert · 2026-10-29

小样本观察不能证明 GEO 效果。主记录跨日（2026-10-29 / 2026-10-30）；ChatGPT Search 的思考强度包含 Extra High / Medium，条件有变化；ChatGPT 正文采集曾失败，read_thread 仅提供摘要 / 预览；已观察到个性化；历史采集的未映射引用已保留于证据；已观察到跨测试记忆污染。仅统计已完成主样本，历史尝试不并入。

采集方法、补测与限制
测试条件分别记录。以每条真实提问的日期、模型界面标签、思考强度和界面模式比较条件，不把继续工作或恢复日期当作测试日期。任一已记录条件不同时，重复重合度标为“条件不同，仅描述性比较”；字段未披露时也不认定条件完全相同。条件变化不能归因为某一项 GEO 战术的效果。
补测政策：用户已批准额外补测并保留失败记录。主表保留 12 个计划位置当前选用的真实提问；其余 10 条真实提问单独保存在历史记录。实际提问总数为 12 条主记录 + 10 条历史提问 = 22 次。同一会话的新采集不增加提问数。
补测日期：2026-10-29、2026-10-30。图表只统计主表中 completed 的样本，历史提问不并入；原会话正文恢复后可以重新选用，但保留此前采集失败和诊断更正。

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Best free resources to learn GEO in 2026

Generative Engine Optimization (GEO) is the practice of improving the likelihood that AI-powered search and answer systems — such as ChatGPT, Perplexity, Gemini, and Google AI Overviews — discover, cite, or recommend your content.

My recommendation is to learn GEO through three complementary approaches: official platform documentation, original research, and hands-on experiments with measurable outcomes. GEO is evolving quickly, and many commercial guides promote tactics whose effectiveness has not been independently established.

For a free learning path, I would prioritize the following resources.

1. Best resources at a glance

1. Google Search Central — Best official guidance
Start here
Free · Official documentation · Beginner to intermediate
Explains how Google's AI search features discover and surface content, why conventional SEO still matters, and which popular GEO claims Google does not endorse.
Read Google's 2026 AI optimization guide
https://developers.google.com/search/docs/fundamentals/ai-optimization-guide

2. The original GEO research paper — Best research foundation
Free · Academic research · Intermediate
The foundational paper introduces a benchmark for measuring visibility in generative-engine responses and evaluates different content optimization strategies.
Read the paper on arXiv
https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.09735

3. LearnGEO — Best structured course
Free · 19 modules · 57 lessons
A structured, self-paced course covering how AI answer systems source information and how to improve content visibility. The site advertises no account requirement.
Take the course
https://learngeo.io/

4. Ahrefs — Best practical SEO-to-GEO bridge
Free learning materials · Practitioner-oriented
Useful for understanding content strategy, search intent, backlinks, authority, and the conventional SEO fundamentals that underpin AI visibility.
Explore Ahrefs' learning resources
https://ahrefs.com/academy

5. Bing Webmaster Tools — Best for measurement
Free · Requires access to a website for its own performance data
Useful for learning how to inspect AI-related visibility and citations where supported. It helps turn GEO from a writing exercise into a measurement problem.
Open Bing Webmaster Tools
https://www.bing.com/webmasters/

6. Apollo Digital Learn GEO — Best short implementation course
Free · 7 lessons · About 100 minutes
A compact introduction to GEO strategy, content readiness, AI citations, and a repeatable measurement workflow. It is agency-produced, so validate its recommendations against platform documentation and your own experiments.

These resources serve different purposes. Google is the strongest source for Google's own requirements; academic papers provide research methods and evidence; courses offer a more convenient route to implementation. No single resource establishes a universal formula for getting recommended by every AI system.

2. Additional resources worth bookmarking

These are useful once you have the basics.

Semrush Academy
Free AI Search courses covering visibility strategy and practical workflows. An account is required.

The GEO Community
Practitioner-oriented guides and experiment write-ups. Treat its results as individual case studies until you can verify the methodology and reproduce the findings.

Awesome GEO
A public GitHub directory collecting research papers, platform documentation, tools, and implementation guides. Useful as a reference library after the introductory course.

Official visibility measurement
• Google Search Console / AI features: measures impressions from supported Google AI search features.
• Bing Webmaster Tools AI Performance: reports citations, cited pages, and associated grounding queries across supported AI experiences.
Both are free. They measure different surfaces and require a website you can access and verify.

3. What does the research actually support?

The original GEO paper by Aggarwal and colleagues, published at KDD 2024, is especially important because it evaluates optimization tactics experimentally. It reports visibility improvements of up to 40% on its benchmark, with results varying by domain. That is a benchmark result, not a promise of a 40% improvement for every website or AI platform.

Google's 2026 guidance adds an important practical constraint: conventional SEO remains foundational, and Google explicitly cautions against relying on supposed GEO hacks such as unnecessary llms.txt files, artificial mentions, or rigid content-chunking rules.

Use these principles to evaluate any tutorial:
• Evidence: Does it report an actual experiment, sample size, baseline, and outcome?
• Attribution: Can the author distinguish a content change from model updates or changes in search demand?
• Reproducibility: Are the prompts, measurement method, and evaluation period documented?
• Commercial incentives: Is the author selling a tool whose effectiveness the tutorial claims to demonstrate?
• Platform specificity: Does the advice work on multiple systems, or only on one particular search experience?

4. Recommended learning plan: two weeks

For an independent builder, I would spend less time collecting courses and more time publishing and measuring original work.

Days 1–2
Learn the fundamentals
Read Google's guide and complete the Ahrefs AEO course. Write a one-page summary of how AI discovery differs from traditional search.

Days 3–4
Read the original paper
Identify the tested tactics, outcome measures, experimental limitations, and assumptions that might not hold in current AI systems.

Days 5–7
Run a baseline experiment
Select 10–20 questions relevant to your topic. Test them across ChatGPT, Perplexity, and Google AI search where available. Record brand mentions, citations, cited URLs, and competitors.

Days 8–10
Publish one controlled content change
For example, improve a post with original observations, verifiable evidence, and clear sourcing. Keep the topic and other conditions as consistent as possible.

Days 11–14
Re-test and publish your findings
Repeat the same prompts, document changes and variability, and publish a short experiment report. A null result is useful if the method is transparent.

One methodological caution: AI answers can vary between runs. Use repeated tests, a consistent prompt set, and a record of the test date and platform. A simple before-and-after comparison is a useful starting point, but it does not establish causality on its own.

My final shortlist

If you only have time for three resources, choose these:
1. Google Search Central for official platform guidance.
2. The original GEO paper for research-backed methods and limitations.
3. Ahrefs' free AEO course for a structured, practical introduction.

Then start testing those methods on content you actually publish. That will teach you more about what works in your own context than completing several overlapping courses.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Best free resources to learn GEO in 2026

Generative Engine Optimization (GEO) is the practice of improving the likelihood that AI-powered search and answer systems — such as ChatGPT, Perplexity, Gemini and Google AI Overviews — discover, reference and recommend your content.

My recommendation is to learn GEO through three complementary approaches: official platform documentation, research with measurable evidence, and hands-on experiments on content you control. Free courses are useful for structure, but many GEO tactics are still evolving, so practical results matter more than certificates.

1. Best free resources at a glance

Ranked by learning value, credibility and usefulness for independent creators and builders.

1. Google Search Central
Start here
Official documentation

Google's guide to optimizing for generative AI features explains how AI Overviews and AI Mode use Google's search infrastructure, why conventional SEO still matters, and why original, useful content is important. 
Google for Developers
+1

Best for: understanding what Google actually recommends, without relying on SEO vendors' interpretations.

2. Original GEO research paper
Academic evidence

GEO: Generative Engine Optimization by researchers from Princeton and IIT Delhi. This foundational study evaluates optimization strategies in generative search and provides a framework for thinking about citations and source visibility. 
The GEO Community
+1

Best for: understanding where GEO techniques come from, what was actually tested, and why evidence-based content may matter.

3. LearnGEO
Free structured course
No account required

learngeo.io offers 19 modules and 57 lessons, with a stated total duration of 352 minutes. It emphasizes sourced claims, GEO fundamentals and practical implementation. 
LearnGEO

Best for: learning systematically instead of assembling dozens of disconnected articles.

4. Ahrefs — AEO course
Practical SEO foundations

Start with Ahrefs Academy and look for its free Answer Engine Optimization (AEO) course and AI-search materials.

Best for: learning content optimization, search intent, authority and discoverability. AEO and GEO overlap substantially, though course availability and access conditions should be checked.

5. Bing Webmaster Tools
Measurement

Read Microsoft's Bing Webmaster Blog and search for its AI Performance reporting documentation.

Best for: understanding how to monitor citations and visibility in supported AI experiences. Availability of reporting for a specific site or platform depends on Microsoft's current coverage.

6. Awesome GEO
Resource directory

GitHub — Awesome GEO curates research papers, official platform documentation, guides and community resources. 
GitHub

Best for: finding primary sources and expanding your learning as new research appears. Check individual links and publication dates before adopting a recommendation.

2. More free courses and practical guides

These are useful alternatives if you prefer a course-style curriculum or want implementation checklists.

Apollo Digital — Free GEO course

Open course

Seven lessons, approximately 100 minutes, covering fundamentals, technical accessibility, content, authority and measurement. The publisher advertises free templates and tools without registration. 
Apollo Digital

GEO Wiki — Learning resource library

Open resource library

A curated overview of free learning materials, including official documentation, courses, research and measurement tools. Useful for choosing your next topic without committing to a long course. 
GEO Wiki

Class Central — GEO resource directory

Read the 2026 guide

Compares courses, blogs, YouTube channels, manuals and podcasts. Check each linked resource individually because some courses are paid or have access restrictions. 
Class Central

3. The most important measurement resource
Bing AI Performance
Free measurement tool

Official documentation

Microsoft's AI Performance report can show cited URLs, citation trends and the queries associated with content cited in supported AI experiences. It is especially useful for learning how to evaluate GEO outcomes. 
Bing Webmaster Tools
+1

Important limitation: citation counts alone do not establish that a specific optimization caused an improvement, and the report does not cover every AI platform.

4. What the evidence says about GEO

Three principles are worth learning before spending time on optimization tactics.

Original research is more valuable than repeating popular advice. The foundational GEO paper introduced a systematic evaluation framework and reported visibility improvements of up to 40% under its experimental conditions. That result should not be treated as a guaranteed improvement for every site or today's AI systems. 
arXiv

Traditional SEO fundamentals remain important. Google's 2026 guidance emphasizes indexability, crawlability, useful original content and a clear technical structure. 
Google for Developers
+1

Be skeptical of universal GEO hacks. Google explicitly says its search systems do not require llms.txt, special GEO markup or artificially fragmented content. Other AI systems may behave differently, so test individual tactics against the platform you want to influence. 
Google for Developers

5. A practical four-week learning plan

I recommend a small experiment-driven curriculum. You can complete it without buying a GEO tool or running a website with significant traffic.

Week 1 — Understand the foundations

Read Google's guide and the original GEO paper.

Deliverable: a one-page summary distinguishing established evidence, platform-specific recommendations and unproven hypotheses.

Week 2 — Establish a baseline

Choose one topic you publish about regularly. Create 10–20 realistic questions your intended audience might ask, then test them on ChatGPT, Perplexity and Gemini.

Deliverable: a spreadsheet recording brand or author mentions, cited URLs, competitors mentioned and answer differences.

Week 3 — Run a controlled content experiment

Publish or improve one piece of content with original evidence, clear explanations and supporting sources. Keep a comparable page or topic unchanged where possible.

Deliverable: a documented hypothesis, before-and-after observations and limitations.

Week 4 — Analyze and publish your findings

Repeat the same questions under comparable conditions. Track mention rate, citation rate and the sources AI systems select. Where possible, supplement manual tests with webmaster analytics.

Deliverable: a short public case study explaining what changed, what did not, and what you would test next.

Manual tests are noisy because answers can vary with wording, model version and time. Treat early findings as exploratory; repeated observations and suitable comparison groups are needed before making causal claims.

My recommendation

Start with these three resources, in this order:

Google's official GEO guidance — establish the technical and content fundamentals.

The original GEO research paper — understand the evidence and experimental methodology.

LearnGEO — fill in the practical gaps with a structured course.

Then run a small experiment and publish the results. This will build both GEO knowledge and a portfolio of evidence-backed content.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
# What credible experiments show about GEO effectiveness

As of October 2026, the evidence suggests that **GEO tactics can improve visibility in AI-generated answers, but the strength of evidence varies substantially by tactic and study design.** The clearest experimental evidence comes from academic research on content optimization. Large-scale industry studies support the importance of third-party brand mentions, while commercial case studies report substantial improvements after coordinated GEO campaigns.

The central distinction is whether a study demonstrates that a tactic *causes* more AI recommendations or merely shows that brands using that tactic tend to receive more mentions.

## 1\. The strongest evidence, ranked by study design

image_group{"query":["Princeton University computer science research campus"],"aspect_ratio":"3:4","layout":"carousel","num_per_query":1}

(Strongest experimental evidence)

**1\. Princeton researchers — GEO: Generative Engine Optimization (KDD 2024)**

Peer-reviewed academic paper · Published August 2024

The researchers introduced GEO-bench and systematically tested content-optimization strategies across diverse queries and domains. They reported that optimization could improve content visibility in generative-engine responses by **up to 40%**\. Tested approaches included adding relevant citations, statistics and quotations, and improving the presentation of information.

**What it establishes\:** Content-level changes can influence how generative engines represent and surface information under experimental conditions.

**Limitations\:** The headline is a maximum result, not an average guaranteed lift. The outcome is content visibility, which does not necessarily mean more brand recommendations, purchases or conversions.

Source: urlKDD 2024 paper and DOIhttps://doi.org/10.1145/3637528.3671900 · urlPrinceton research recordhttps://collaborate.princeton.edu/en/publications/geo-generative-engine-optimization/\.

---

image_group{"query":["Semrush company logo on white background"],"aspect_ratio":"3:4","layout":"carousel","num_per_query":1}

(Real-world intervention)

**2\. Semrush — Its own AI visibility improvement program**

Company-reported case study · May 2026 retrospective

Semrush reported that its AI share of voice for target prompts increased from **13% to 32% in one month** during summer 2025, nearly tripling its share of answers mentioning the company relative to competitors. It subsequently refined its measurement and optimization process.

**What it establishes\:** A company can measure and materially improve its own visibility over a defined campaign period.

**Limitations\:** This is a first-party case study, not an independently replicated randomized experiment. The reported change does not isolate the causal contribution of individual tactics or rule out changes in competitors, prompts or AI systems.

Source: urlSemrush: How we're driving AI visibilityhttps://www.semrush.com/blog/how-we-are-using-semrush-to-drive-llm-visibility/\.

---

image_group{"query":["Ahrefs SEO software company logo"],"aspect_ratio":"3:4","layout":"carousel","num_per_query":1}

(Large-scale observational evidence)

**3\. Ahrefs — Brand visibility factors across 75,000 brands**

Industry data analysis · 2025

Ahrefs analyzed millions of AI-generated responses and found that brand mentions across the web correlated strongly with mentions in Google's AI Overviews. Its May 2025 study reported a Spearman correlation of **0.664** for web mentions, compared with **0.218** for backlinks and **0.392** for branded search volume.

A later study extended the analysis to ChatGPT, AI Mode and AI Overviews, again finding strong associations between third-party brand mentions and AI visibility.

**What it establishes\:** Off-site brand presence is a promising GEO signal worth testing.

**Limitations\:** Correlation cannot establish whether additional mentions cause AI visibility to rise. Larger, more established brands may naturally have both more mentions and greater AI visibility.

Sources: url2025 studyhttps://ahrefs.com/blog/ai-overview-brand-correlation/ · urlCross-platform follow-up studyhttps://ahrefs.com/blog/ai-brand-visibility-correlations/\.

## 2\. What does the evidence say about individual GEO tactics?

The studies above support different conclusions. The table below separates tactics with experimental support from those with mainly observational or commercial evidence.

| GEO tactic | Evidence | Current conclusion |
|--------|--------|--------|
| Add statistics, citations and expert quotations | Princeton controlled evaluation | Promising content-level intervention; test in your own niche |
| Earn third-party brand mentions | Large-scale Ahrefs correlation analysis | Strong association; causal effect remains uncertain |
| Get included in “best of” lists and comparison articles | Commercial case studies and AI citation analyses | Promising for product recommendations; limited controlled evidence |
| Publish more pages | Ahrefs observational analysis | Weak evidence that page volume alone increases AI visibility |
| Build backlinks | Ahrefs observational analysis | Weaker association than brand mentions in its 2025 AI Overview study |
| Publish social content | Comparative AI search research | Effects vary by platform; social content may be less influential than earned media |

Two additional sources help explain these findings.

**4\. Chen et al. — *Generative Engine Optimization: How to Dominate AI Search* \(2025)**

This arXiv preprint uses large-scale controlled experiments to compare AI search with traditional search across verticals, languages and query variations. It finds that AI search systems favor earned media—third-party authoritative sources—over brand-owned and social content, with substantial differences between engines. urlRead the paperhttps://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08919\.

The distinction matters: this research provides evidence about *which sources AI systems tend to use*, but it does not establish that purchasing or securing a particular media placement will cause a brand to receive more recommendations.

**5\. Smoking Gun PR — Four-month GEO campaign**

A home-appliance brand's campaign combined editorial coverage, expert commentary, product comparison articles and community placements. The agency reported\:

AI share of voice

# 3.2% → 9.1%

Brand mentions

# 274 → 1,424

Reported results over four months; the campaign combined several tactics.

Source: urlSmoking Gun PR case studyhttps://smokinggun.agency/our-work/geo-casestudy/\.

This is useful real-world evidence, but it is agency-reported, lacks a published control group and does not isolate which intervention drove the results.

## 3\. How to interpret the evidence correctly

Three measurement distinctions are essential when evaluating GEO claims.

**Brand mention rate**

How frequently an AI answer names the brand. This is the most direct measure of whether a brand appears at all.

**Recommendation and position**

Whether the AI actually recommends the brand, places it among the top options, or merely mentions it in passing. A citation alone does not establish a positive recommendation.

**Business impact**

Whether the intervention increases qualified visits, leads or purchases. Greater AI visibility does not automatically translate into commercial results.

AI answers can also change across repeated runs of the same prompt. Semrush explicitly describes this non-determinism in its measurement discussion. Consequently, before-and-after comparisons based on only a handful of prompts can produce misleading conclusions. urlSource and methodology discussionhttps://www.semrush.com/blog/how-we-are-using-semrush-to-drive-llm-visibility/\.

## 4\. What would constitute convincing evidence?

For someone conducting independent GEO experiments, I would prioritize the following design.

1. **Establish a baseline.** Select 30–100 realistic prompts in one topic area and record brand mentions, recommendation rate, ranking within recommendations and citations.
2. **Change one variable at a time.** For example, test a structured, evidence-rich article against a comparable article without the optimization. Alternatively, compare prompts associated with earned-media placements against a matched control group.
3. **Repeat across multiple runs.** Use the same prompts, models, settings and observation schedule. Record changes in the AI system and its retrieval sources.
4. **Measure the treatment effect.** Compare changes in the treated group with changes in the control group, ideally using a difference-in-differences design. For content experiments, account for differences in topic, baseline authority and publication timing.
5. **Publish the results, including failures.** Share the prompt set, intervention, sample size, observation period, outcome definitions and uncertainty. This makes the findings independently testable.

## Bottom line

The strongest conclusion currently supported by the evidence is that **GEO is measurable and content optimization can improve AI visibility, but reliable causal evidence for many popular tactics remains limited**\.

For practical experimentation, I would start with two hypotheses\:

1. **Content-level optimization\:** Does adding concrete statistics, attributable citations and useful comparisons increase the probability that an AI answer surfaces your content or mentions your brand?
2. **Earned-media optimization\:** Does getting your brand included in relevant third-party comparisons increase its recommendation rate relative to a matched control?

These hypotheses connect the academic evidence to real-world brand outcomes, and both can be tested without relying on vendor claims or assuming that correlation proves causation.

---

If you want, I can:

- Suggest a detailed plan for running a GEO experiment to test brand mention increase
- Provide example prompts and control set for a GEO brand mention experiment
- Explain how to distinguish AI mentions vs positive brand recommendations in GEO testing

1www.axios.com GEO starts with the question, not the answer 昨天 — A new Muck Rack and Generative Pulse study suggests companies should begin generative engine optimization (GEO) by understanding what their audiences ask, rather than simply trying to secure AI citations. Researchers tested 1,020 mostly general consumer prompts across ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Perplexity, finding that cited sources varied substantially by question and platform. The results challenge the idea that news coverage is always the most reliable path to AI visibility. Citation patterns were also inconsistent over time: when researchers repeated the same questions on ChatGPT on the same day, only 41% of cited domains overlapped, making isolated tests a poor measure of success. The study combined prompt testing with broader monitoring of mostly brand-related queries, covering more than 40 million responses. Its central takeaway is that GEO is unpredictable and continually changing. Communications teams should prioritize the information their audiences seek and assess visibility across relevant questions and platforms, rather than investing in content solely because they hope a particular AI system will cite it.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
What credible experiments show about GEO and AI brand visibility

As of October 30, 2026, the evidence supports a qualified conclusion: GEO tactics can influence what AI systems cite and recommend under controlled conditions, but reliable, lasting increases in a real brand’s organic visibility across AI search engines remain difficult to establish.

The key is to distinguish three outcomes:

Brand mentions: Does ChatGPT, Gemini, Claude, or Perplexity name a brand?

Citations: Does the answer link to a brand’s website or a third-party page mentioning it?

Recommendations: Does the AI actually recommend the brand or product to the user?

These metrics are related, but an improvement in one does not establish an improvement in the others.

1. The strongest evidence, ranked by experimental rigor
1. Princeton University — GEO: Generative Engine Optimization (2024)
Peer-reviewed research

Finding: GEO methods improved visibility by up to 40% in the researchers’ experimental setup. 
Princeton University
+1

The researchers introduced GEO-bench, a benchmark of queries and relevant web sources, and compared optimized content with unoptimized content. Methods included adding relevant statistics, quotations, authoritative citations, and other content modifications.

Important limitation: The experiment operated within a controlled retrieval-and-generation pipeline. It demonstrates that changing content can improve how a retrieved source is represented in an AI answer. It does not establish that the same edits will make a brand more likely to appear in organic ChatGPT or Google AI search results.

Source: Aggarwal et al., KDD 2024. Paper and publication details

2. Competitive GEO in AI Answer Engines (May 2026)
Large controlled experiment

Finding: Topical relevance and the position of a source in the provided context were the strongest drivers of being cited first. Explicit pricing and recent timestamps also helped consistently. 
arXiv
+1

The researchers ran 252,000 trials across six LLMs, using paired comparisons in which candidate documents differed by one of 18 content factors. Brand anonymization and counterbalanced source order helped isolate the effects of content from brand and position biases.

Why it matters: This is unusually strong evidence for specific citation-selection mechanisms. It also suggests that superficial formatting changes alone are unlikely to be a reliable GEO strategy.

Limitation: The researchers supplied the competing documents directly to the model. The study primarily measures citation selection after retrieval, not whether publishing a page causes an AI search engine to discover it.

Source: What Gets Cited: Competitive GEO in AI Answer Engines — preprint.

3. What Drives AI Citations? (October 2026)
Recent empirical research

Finding: Pages whose titles and paragraphs match users’ questions are cited more often. The authors also found that retrieval behavior explains substantially more variation than page-level characteristics alone. 
arXiv

The study examined two 90-day collections covering 158,099 answers and 468,037 citations across commercial queries. It combined observational analysis with API-based replay experiments.

Its central insight is that citation visibility depends heavily on which pages the engine retrieves for its own reformulated queries. Brand familiarity also matters: models sometimes name brands without a supporting page being cited.

Why it matters: It separates two different optimization problems: getting a page retrieved and getting a brand mentioned. Improving on-page content may help with the first, but cannot guarantee the second.

Source: What Drives AI Citations? Content, Retrieval and Model Knowledge in GEO — preprint.

4. SafeGEO — AI product recommendations (2026)
Controlled recommendation study

Finding: GEO-style manipulations increased the rate at which targeted, flawed products entered recommendation sets by up to 83.2 percentage points in the tested scenarios.

The researchers constructed 600 recommendation cases and tested 22 manipulation variants.

Why it matters: It shows that content manipulation can affect actual recommendations, a more consequential outcome than citation frequency alone.

Limitation: This is a study of recommendation vulnerabilities under controlled conditions, including potentially misleading content. It does not establish that ordinary, truthful optimization will produce comparable gains in commercial AI search.

Source: SafeGEO: Understanding Generative Engine Optimization Risks in Recommendation Agents — preprint.

2. What real-world brand studies tell us

The controlled studies above isolate specific mechanisms. These additional studies examine brands and live AI search systems, but their findings are mostly correlational or observational.

GEO at Scale, June 2026

	

100,000+ AI responses across 100+ brands. Initial visibility differed sharply by brand maturity: 73% for major household names, 44% for established mid-market brands, and 11% for niche brands. 
数字对象标识符

	

Brand stature is an important baseline factor; the study does not prove that any particular GEO tactic causes growth.




Search Engine Land, September 2026

	

Two practical experiments tracked 15 commercial-intent keywords across multiple AI platforms, logging 775 citation events. The first campaign saw listicles and PR placements account for most citations. 
Search Engine Land

	

Third-party placements may be valuable, but the uncontrolled campaign design cannot isolate their causal effect.

The Ahrefs results are useful because of their scale. However, correlation coefficients cannot tell us whether creating more brand mentions will increase AI visibility. Already-famous brands may receive more mentions and more AI recommendations for the same underlying reason: existing brand recognition.

The June 2026 GEO at Scale paper is similarly useful as a baseline. Its authors explicitly propose further protocols to test causal interventions rather than claiming that the descriptive findings prove which tactics work. 
数字对象标识符

3. Which GEO tactics have the best evidence?

Improve topical relevance and answerability

Strongest direct evidence

Match page titles and substantive paragraphs to real user questions. Make claims specific, relevant, and easy to verify. Controlled citation experiments support relevance as a meaningful factor. 
arXiv
+1

Provide concrete facts, pricing, and current information

Controlled evidence

Explicit prices and recent timestamps improved citation selection in the competitive GEO experiment. The size and transferability of those gains in live organic search remain uncertain. 
arXiv

Earn third-party mentions, reviews, and listicle placements

Promising, not yet causal

Real-world studies show associations between brand mentions and AI visibility, and campaign reports show third-party listicles can attract citations. Robust evidence that deliberately acquiring such placements causes sustained cross-platform visibility gains is still limited. 
Ahrefs
+1

Add statistics, quotations, and authoritative citations

Context-dependent

These techniques improved visibility in the original GEO benchmark. Their effectiveness depends on the domain and experimental setup; they should not be treated as universal ranking factors. 
Princeton University
+1

Assume that more pages, FAQ blocks, schema markup, or formatting automatically increase mentions

Insufficient causal evidence. These changes may help with accessibility, retrieval, or interpretation in particular contexts, but their independent effect on brand mentions and recommendations has not been established consistently.

4. Why the evidence is harder to interpret than it looks

Three methodological issues explain why many GEO success stories overstate what their data proves.

1. Retrieval versus generation. If a page is never retrieved, improving its formatting may have no effect. A test that gives a page directly to an LLM isolates generation behavior but does not measure organic discoverability.

2. Stochastic outputs. The same question can yield different answers, citations, and recommendations across runs. A study published in April 2026 specifically recommends repeated measurements rather than judging visibility from a single response. 
arXiv

3. Confounding and competition. A brand might gain visibility because it became more famous, earned more media coverage, or improved its traditional search presence. And even if one brand gains recommendations, competitors may lose them. Controlled recommendation research highlights this competitive effect. 
alphaXiv
+1

A particularly relevant recent finding comes from an Axios report published October 28, 2026, covering a Muck Rack and Generative Pulse study. Across repeated ChatGPT queries on the same day, only 41% of cited domains overlapped. This illustrates why single-run comparisons are unreliable. 
Axios

5. How to test GEO credibly yourself

For an independent builder testing whether a tactic improves a brand's AI visibility [personal context omitted], I would use the following protocol.

A small, defensible GEO experiment

Define one intervention. For example, publish a substantive third-party article about your work, or rewrite a page to answer a specific user question. Avoid changing several things at once.

Pre-register 20–50 relevant prompts. Include branded queries, non-branded discovery queries, and recommendation queries. Keep their wording fixed for the primary analysis.

Establish a baseline. Run each prompt multiple times across the selected AI engines and record brand mentions, recommendation inclusion, citations, and cited URLs.

Use controls. Compare treated pages or topics with similar untreated pages or topics. Where practical, stagger publication dates to help separate intervention effects from general time trends.

Measure over time. Repeat the observations for at least several weeks. Record engine version, date, location settings, and whether web search was enabled.

Report uncertainty. Calculate changes in mention and recommendation rates, not just raw counts. Include variation across runs, platforms, and prompt categories.

The primary metric should be the change in the proportion of eligible prompts that mention or recommend the brand, compared with the baseline and a suitable control. Citation rate should be reported separately.

Bottom line

The most defensible conclusions from the available evidence are:

Content relevance and source position can influence citation selection in controlled tests.

Brand mentions, third-party coverage, and YouTube presence correlate with AI visibility, but the causal effect of increasing these signals remains uncertain.

Recommendation manipulation can change model outputs, although that does not establish that legitimate marketing tactics will yield equivalent gains.

No single tactic has yet been shown convincingly to produce stable, sustained improvements in organic brand recommendations across major AI platforms.

For further reading, start with the Princeton GEO paper, then the competitive citation experiment and the October 2026 retrieval study. Together, they provide a stronger foundation than vendor playbooks based solely on correlation or isolated success stories.

18www.axios.comGEO starts with the question, not the answer今天 — A new Muck Rack and Generative Pulse study suggests companies should begin generative engine optimization (GEO) by understanding what their audiences ask, rather than simply trying to secure AI citations. Researchers tested 1,020 mostly general consumer prompts across ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Perplexity, finding that cited sources varied substantially by question and platform. The results challenge the idea that news coverage is always the most reliable path to AI visibility. Citation patterns were also inconsistent over time: when researchers repeated the same questions on ChatGPT on the same day, only 41% of cited domains overlapped, making isolated tests a poor measure of success. The study combined prompt testing with broader monitoring of mostly brand-related queries, covering more than 40 million responses. Its central takeaway is that GEO is unpredictable and continually changing. Communications teams should prioritize the information their audiences seek and assess visibility across relevant questions and platforms, rather than investing in content solely because they hope a particular AI system will cite it.

33www.architecturaldigest.comIs Your Business Ready for the AI Era?2026年7月6日 — The rise of AI-powered search is transforming how clients discover architecture and design firms. With Google making AI Overviews its default search mode and generative AI adoption growing rapidly, businesses must adapt their marketing strategies to remain visible. Generative engine optimization (GEO), also called answer engine optimization (AEO), focuses on getting firms cited in AI-generated answers rather than simply ranking highly in traditional search results. Marketing agencies are responding by offering AI audits that measure brand visibility across platforms such as ChatGPT, Claude, Perplexity, Gemini, and Google AI Overviews. These assessments identify where firms appear in responses to relevant customer queries and where competitors outperform them. To improve visibility, businesses should ensure their websites are accessible to AI crawlers, organize information clearly, use structured data, and publish factual project descriptions highlighting measurable outcomes. Credibility is equally important: earned media coverage, industry awards, and reputable third-party editorial content help establish authority. LinkedIn and Reddit can also influence AI-generated recommendations, provided engagement remains authentic. Because GEO practices are still evolving, firms should experiment and continually refine their strategies. Practical first steps include checking website crawlability, sharing achievements on LinkedIn, pursuing press coverage, and rewriting website content to answer prospective clients' questions clearly and factually.

34www.theatlantic.comYour Search Results Are Getting Sloptimized2026年6月10日 — As AI chatbots and Google’s AI search answers replace traditional links, companies are trying to optimize for recommendations—a practice dubbed “sloptimization” or generative-engine optimization. Shopify, for example, has published dozens of rankings that place its own e-commerce platform first, and similar self-promotional lists from Figma and ClickUp may influence chatbot answers. Research suggests these tactics can work because AI systems may not distinguish brand-written rankings from independent reviews. Marketers are also planting favorable mentions on Reddit, whose posts are frequently cited by chatbots even when they have few upvotes, and embedding hidden instructions in content—a practice Microsoft calls AI recommendation poisoning. Meanwhile, AI-generated answers reduce traffic to independent publishers, and recipe creators fear their work will be copied or distorted. Google says it fights manipulation and favors genuinely useful content; experts warn that spam can eventually trigger penalties. Still, businesses are likely to keep experimenting while the tactics sometimes succeed. The trend reflects a web increasingly designed for machines rather than people, with future AI agents potentially making decisions and purchases on users’ behalf.

41www.itpro.comWill a generative engine optimization manager be your next big hire?2026年2月11日 — Generative engine optimization (GEO) is emerging as a new marketing discipline as businesses adapt to consumers increasingly using AI tools such as ChatGPT and Claude to find information. Companies including Adobe, Apple, Caterpillar and Capital One have begun hiring GEO managers, strategists or specialists to improve their visibility in AI-generated answers. Unlike traditional search engine optimization (SEO), which focuses on search rankings and keywords, GEO aims to ensure that AI systems can easily understand, extract and reuse a company's information. The role builds on SEO foundations while emphasizing website architecture, machine-readable data, content credibility, knowledge graph management and collaboration across departments. GEO managers need technical knowledge of website crawlability and AI systems, strong data stewardship skills, and the ability to coordinate developers, content teams and other stakeholders. They must ensure information is accurate, trustworthy and structured for AI discovery. Investment is growing rapidly: Conductor's 2026 report found that 97% of 250 digital leaders saw positive GEO impacts in 2025, while 94% planned to increase AI-search investment in 2026, allocating an average of 12% of marketing budgets. Although GEO will not replace SEO, companies that develop their existing SEO capabilities into GEO strategies may gain a competitive advantage as AI-driven discovery becomes more widespread.

42www.wsj.comHow Businesses Are Manipulating ChatGPT Results2026年1月30日 — Businesses are increasingly paying specialists to influence ChatGPT and other AI chatbots through generative engine optimization (GEO), an emerging practice similar to traditional search engine optimization (SEO). As chatbot recommendations drive growing numbers of customers to businesses, appearing prominently in AI-generated answers has become commercially valuable. Similarweb reports that chatbot links directed users to websites more than 230 million times monthly by September 2025, triple the figure from a year earlier. These visitors also tend to spend more time on websites and are more likely to make purchases than visitors arriving through Google. GEO practitioners manipulate AI recommendations by strategically placing favorable descriptions and superlatives across multiple websites. For example, companies may repeatedly associate their products with phrases such as “highest-rated for sciatica” to influence chatbot responses. Traditional search rankings and mentions in authoritative publications can also affect AI recommendations, while obscure products and businesses are particularly vulnerable to manipulation because AI models have less established knowledge about them. Although OpenAI and Google employ safeguards against manipulation, both acknowledge that protecting their systems remains an ongoing challenge. Consumers should therefore treat AI recommendations cautiously and verify important advice through independent reviews, trusted news sources, or qualified human experts.

48www.businessinsider.comThe new SEO is GEO &mdash;&nbsp;and no one really knows the rules2025年11月19日 — Generative engine optimization (GEO) is emerging as AI chatbots increasingly influence how consumers discover and evaluate brands, but its rules remain uncertain. About half of US consumers use AI-powered search, while only 16% of brands systematically track their performance. The field has created a rush of agencies, consultants, and tools, including Adobe’s planned $1.9 billion acquisition of Semrush, alongside concerns about exaggerated claims and “snake oil” services. Unlike traditional SEO, GEO lacks historical data, standardized measurement, and stability because AI responses are personalized and models are frequently retrained. Google, Microsoft, and Perplexity say established SEO fundamentals—accurate, fresh, trustworthy, human-focused content, sound website structure, schema, sitemaps, and multimedia—remain essential. Microsoft additionally recommends question-and-answer formats, lists, tables, and IndexNow. Companies should focus on user intent and content that can be synthesized into answers rather than chasing temporary ranking tricks. RealSense’s experience shows that correcting misinformation, improving FAQs, earning credible coverage, and building executive visibility can improve AI-generated brand perceptions. Ultimately, GEO appears less a separate discipline than an evolving extension of SEO, public relations, and broader brand building.

53nymag.comSEO Is Dead. Say Hello to GEO.2025年8月4日 — The rise of AI chatbots is disrupting search-engine optimization (SEO), an industry that shaped online publishing and helped businesses attract visitors through Google. As chatbots increasingly answer questions directly, websites are losing traffic, prompting marketers to embrace generative-engine optimization (GEO): strategies to make brands and content more likely to appear in AI-generated answers. Emerging advice includes publishing concise, clearly authored, authoritative material in easily excerpted formats such as lists, tables, rankings, diagrams, and original research. Mentions on trusted, frequently crawled sites like Wikipedia, Reddit, and YouTube, along with broader public relations, may also improve visibility. Startups are building tools to track brand mentions and generate AI-optimized content, though experts warn that some tactics—such as AI-generated backlinks or hidden instructions—are scams or short-lived tricks. Several specialists argue that useful, high-quality content remains essential, with AI assisting research and drafting under human oversight. The transition is uncertain: chatbot referrals may not compensate for declining search traffic, and AI platforms could capture user relationships and monetize them through advertising. GEO may offer marketers new opportunities, but competition for visibility is likely to intensify.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
For an independent builder, I would prioritize GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) resources that teach you to establish a baseline, run a small experiment, measure AI mentions and citations, and decide what to change next. Free worksheets and reproducible research are more useful than generic lists of ranking tips.

My strongest starting points are Aleyda Solís for practical workflows, Ahrefs for structured tutorials and large-scale data, and MLforSEO for an evidence-labeled checklist. Seer Interactive and Search Engine Land are useful when you want to learn how practitioners test specific GEO hypotheses.

1. Best publishers to learn from
1. Aleyda Solís
Best for independent builders
Free worksheets

What she publishes: AI Search Optimization Checklist, prompt-library workbook, and frameworks for measuring AI presence, diagnosing gaps, and connecting visibility to business impact.

Why it stands out: Her September 2026 checklist lays out a workflow from choosing important customer journeys to measuring whether optimization works. Her prompt-library tutorial explains how to create a representative set of prompts and turn observations into actions. Both are tool-agnostic. 
aleydasolis.com
+1

Best use: Build your first GEO measurement spreadsheet and a repeatable monthly testing routine.

2. Ahrefs
Best structured learning path
Free guides

What it publishes: An eight-chapter Answer Engine Optimization guide, covering how AI answers work, visibility measurement, prompt selection, content optimization, brand mentions, technical accessibility, and inaccurate AI answers.

Why it stands out: Ahrefs says the guide draws on its own data from millions of prompts and responses. Its 75,000-brand visibility study examines relationships between AI mentions and signals such as YouTube mentions, web mentions, and domain authority. The study explicitly warns that correlation does not establish causation. 
Ahrefs
+1

Best use: Learn the end-to-end workflow, then use its research to formulate hypotheses for your own account or website. You can start with the free educational content without committing to a paid tool.

3. MLforSEO
Best implementation checklist
Free Google Sheets

What it publishes: Lazarina Stoy's 44-check GEO checklist, covering crawler access, indexing, entity clarity, content extractability, evidence, and measurement.

Why it stands out: Each check includes an evidence label distinguishing platform-documented guidance, research-backed findings, practitioner practice, and unconfirmed claims. The resource includes a prompt tracker, progress dashboard, and links to supporting articles and official documentation. 
MLforSEO

Best use: Audit a small website or landing page without buying a GEO analytics subscription. It also helps prevent unverified SEO folklore from becoming part of your workflow.

4. Seer Interactive
Original experiments
Large datasets

What it publishes: Original GEO experiments and AI search studies, including its 2024 generative-search optimization study.

Why it stands out: The study benchmarked more than 10,000 search terms and reported improvements from particular content interventions, while finding that keyword stuffing and authoritative-sounding language did not significantly help. Its newer GEO research hub includes further experiments and guidance on assessing questionable claims of rapid visibility gains. 
seerinteractive.com
+1

Best use: Learn to formulate controlled tests and evaluate effect sizes. Treat individual findings as conditional on their experimental setup, particularly when applying older research to today's AI systems.

5. Search Engine Land
Hands-on experiments
Actionable case studies

What it publishes: Practitioner articles such as “3 GEO experiments you should try this year” and “Two GEO experiments challenge conventional AI visibility advice”.

Why it stands out: These articles show how to test content formats, investigate brand representation, identify sources AI systems cite, and monitor whether placements continue to appear. A 2026 case study reports that approximately half of some sources stopped being cited within 30 days, illustrating why repeated measurement matters. 
Search Engine Land
+1

Best use: Find a concrete experiment to replicate each week. Check sample size, measurement window, and whether the author can distinguish an observed change from a causal effect.

6. Peec AI

Best for continuously discovering new GEO hypotheses.

Its research blog publishes studies on citation sources, prompt tracking, query fan-outs, and content optimization. One study analyzed 5.7 million data points from nearly 200,000 AI responses across eight engines. (Peec AI) 
Peec AI
+1

Limitation: Peec sells AI visibility analytics, so its research is commercially motivated. Use it to identify experiments worth running, and inspect the underlying methodology before accepting a claimed effect.

7. Kevin Indig

Best for research synthesis and strategic reasoning.

His Growth Memo examines organic growth, AI search, and the evidence behind optimization strategies. Examples include a comparison of ten AI Mode studies and research on how much AI responses can be influenced. Some deeper articles require a paid subscription. 
growth-memo.com
+2

Best use: Understand why a tactic might work, where the evidence is weak, and how to prioritize experiments rather than following every new GEO trend.

8. Canlah AI
Open-data playbook
Reusable under CC BY 4.0

Best for learning from a small, inspectable dataset.

Its free GEO Playbook documents an experiment using 110 Singapore buyer questions, 141 ChatGPT answers, and 917 citation records. The data is published openly, and the playbook is licensed under Creative Commons Attribution 4.0. 
Canlah AI

Limitation: The initial dataset is a snapshot from September 23, 2026, with a Singapore focus. It is useful for learning to inspect citations and reproduce analyses, but it cannot establish universal GEO rules.

2. Which sources should you prioritize?

For a solo creator or independent builder, I would use this reading order:

Priority

3. An important qualification about GEO evidence

A tutorial can be data-backed without proving that its recommended tactic reliably causes more AI mentions.

Two findings illustrate the distinction:

A study reported by Axios on October 28, 2026, found that repeated ChatGPT queries on the same day shared only 41% of cited domains. A single before-and-after observation can therefore be misleading. 
Axios

A 2026 research survey covering 45 GEO studies concluded that evidence for stable, long-term, cross-platform causal improvements in organic discoverability remains limited. 
arXiv

When evaluating any tutorial, look for four things: a defined baseline, a repeatable protocol, an appropriate comparison or control, and results measured across multiple runs. Treat large correlation studies as evidence for hypotheses, not proof that a specific intervention will work for your account.

4. The best fit for your current learning direction

[Personal context omitted.] I would start with Aleyda Solís + Ahrefs + Search Engine Land.

Use Aleyda's workflow to define a small set of questions relevant to the audience you want to reach. Use Ahrefs and Peec AI to generate hypotheses, then use Search Engine Land's experiments as examples of how to test them.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Who publishes the best practical, data-backed GEO tutorials for independent builders?

As of October 2026, I would prioritize independent SEO researchers, experimental marketing teams, and practitioners who publish reproducible experiments over generic GEO agencies.

There is an important distinction between three types of content:

Experimental research: Tests whether a particular GEO tactic actually changes AI visibility.

Implementation tutorials: Shows how to execute GEO improvements on a real website or personal brand.

Industry analysis: Explains how ChatGPT, Perplexity, Gemini and Google AI Overviews discover and recommend sources.

For an independent builder, the ideal learning source combines the first two.

1. My recommended publishers

1. Seer Interactive — Best for controlled experiments

Highest priority

Research team / SEO agency · Free research

Publishes experiments testing individual GEO variables.

Focuses on whether changes to content, author information, schema, freshness and page structure affect AI visibility.

Its experimental approach is useful because it attempts to isolate individual factors instead of changing everything simultaneously.

Why follow: This is closest to the scientific method. You can reproduce its experiments on your own website.

Start with: Seer Interactive experiments and case studies. The article describes its AI Lab and controlled testing methodology.

2. Ahrefs — Best for large-scale quantitative research

Highest priority

SEO software company · Extensive free research

Analyzed 75,000 brands across millions of AI responses.

Examines relationships between brand mentions, YouTube presence, backlinks and AI visibility.

Publishes practical guides on monitoring ChatGPT mentions and identifying third-party sources.

Why follow: Its research provides useful quantitative evidence and actionable ideas for content distribution.

Recommended reading:

Top Brand Visibility Factors in ChatGPT, AI Mode, and AI Overviews — 75,000 brands.

How to Monitor Brand Mentions in ChatGPT — includes a free manual monitoring approach.

AI Search Strategy: 4 Pillars to Show Up More — practical strategy.

Important limitation: Most of its large-scale findings are correlations, not causal experiments.

3. Kevin Indig / Growth Memo — Best for understanding AI recommendations

Highest priority

Independent growth researcher · Newsletter and research

Studies how brands appear in AI answers and how users respond to recommendations.

Distinguishes brand mentions, citations, visibility and actual business outcomes.

Collaborates with Semrush on large-scale research.

Why follow: Many GEO practitioners optimize for citations without understanding whether AI actually recommends their brand. Kevin's work addresses this distinction.

Recommended reading:

AI Halftime Report H1 2026.

AI Visibility Is a Topic-Level Game.

The Ghost Citations Study — 3,981 domain appearances across 115 prompts and 14 countries.

Particularly useful insight: appearing as a cited source does not guarantee that your brand is mentioned in the answer.

4. Aleyda Solis — Best for structured learning and implementation

Highest priority

Independent SEO consultant · Free learning resources

Created a free AI Search Optimization learning roadmap.

Covers GEO fundamentals, content optimization, prompt research, technical SEO, brand authority and measurement.

Curates resources from multiple practitioners rather than restricting learning to her own material.

Why follow: She provides a structured curriculum, reducing the time spent searching for disconnected tutorials.

Recommended resources:

Learning AI Search — Free Roadmap.

Aleyda's AI Search Articles.

AI Search Optimization Framework and Worksheets.

5. Search Engine Land — Best for practitioner experiments and industry developments

Industry publication · Free articles

Publishes practitioner-led experiments, technical analyses and case studies.

Provides detailed accounts of what worked and what failed.

Useful for discovering emerging tactics and checking whether conventional GEO advice holds up.

Why follow: It offers a broader range of real-world experiments than most individual newsletters.

Start with: Two GEO Experiments Challenge Conventional AI Visibility Advice (September 2026).

The article documents two experiments, including a 30-day cold-start SaaS experiment, and reports the relative contribution of listicles, PR and guest posts.

6. Princeton University — Best for foundational scientific evidence

Academic research · Open research paper

Published the foundational GEO paper at ACM KDD 2024.

Introduced GEO-Bench and a framework for measuring visibility in generative engine responses.

Experimented with different content optimization methods.

Why follow: It provides a research foundation for evaluating GEO tactics instead of relying on marketing claims.

Start with: GEO: Generative Engine Optimization (KDD 2024).

The paper reported visibility improvements of up to 40% in its experimental setting. This result should not be interpreted as a guaranteed 40% improvement in real-world AI recommendations.

3. Additional resources worth monitoring

These are useful, although I would assign them lower priority than the six above.

Semrush — AI Search Research. Particularly useful for large datasets and research conducted with Kevin Indig. Its 2026 ghost-citation study is a good example. 
Semrush
+1

The GEO Community — Resource directory. Useful for discovering new tutorials, research and practitioners. Evaluate individual contributions rather than assuming everything is independently verified. 
The GEO Community

GitHub — Awesome GEO. A community-maintained directory of research, documentation and tools. Useful for finding open-source projects. 
GitHub

4. My suggested learning combination

[Personal context omitted.] I would avoid following all these publishers equally.

I would use four as the core learning sources:

Recommended learning stack

Aleyda Solis — Learn the framework

Follow the roadmap and understand the different components of GEO.

Ahrefs — Understand the data

Study large-scale research and identify potentially useful optimization factors.

Seer Interactive — Reproduce experiments

Select individual tactics, formulate hypotheses and run controlled tests.

Kevin Indig — Understand the business impact

Learn how to distinguish AI citations, brand recommendations and actual user behavior.

5. One important research principle

GEO is still an evolving field. A lot of published advice is based on correlations, small case studies or vendor-reported results.

For example:

Ahrefs' research involving 75,000 brands identifies strong correlations between brand mentions and AI visibility. It cannot establish that deliberately increasing mentions will cause higher visibility. 
Ahrefs

The Princeton study demonstrates improvements under controlled experimental conditions, with limitations when transferring findings to live AI search systems. 
数字对象标识符

Recent academic reviews also highlight substantial variation across platforms, prompts and measurement methods. 
arXiv

I would use the following evidence hierarchy when evaluating a GEO tutorial:

Controlled experiments with control groups.

Repeated experiments with disclosed datasets and methodology.

Large-scale observational research.

Case studies with baseline, timeframe and measurable outcomes.

Practitioner opinions and unverified recommendations.

My overall recommendation: Build your learning around Aleyda + Ahrefs + Seer, and use Kevin Indig to connect the technical work with business outcomes.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
The best free resources for learning Generative Engine Optimization (GEO) in 2026 combine foundational research, official platform guidance, structured courses, curated learning paths, and practitioner content. GEO focuses on making content more likely to be retrieved, cited, or mentioned in AI-generated answers (ChatGPT, Perplexity, Google AI Overviews/AI Mode, Gemini, etc.), overlapping heavily with Answer Engine Optimization (AEO) and traditional SEO fundamentals.⁠Stackmatix

Foundational Research (Start Here)
Original GEO paper: GEO: Generative Engine Optimization by Aggarwal et al. (arXiv:2311.09735, published at KDD 2024). This is the primary source that named the field. It defines visibility metrics, introduces GEO-bench, and shows that certain content strategies can increase citation/visibility rates by ~30–40%. Free to read on arXiv.⁠arXiv
Follow-up research (e.g., comparative studies across engines like ChatGPT, Claude, Perplexity, and Gemini) is also available on arXiv for deeper technical context.
Official Documentation
Google Search Central: “Optimizing your website for generative AI features on Google Search” (and related pages on AI features/AI Overviews). Google’s position is that standard SEO best practices (helpful, reliable, people-first content; technical eligibility for indexing and snippets) still apply. There are no special requirements or “GEO hacks” (such as llms.txt) needed for AI Overviews or AI Mode. This is the authoritative baseline for Google’s ecosystem.⁠Developers.google
Bing Webmaster Tools also provides free AI citation and visibility insights.
Free Structured Courses

These are practical, self-paced, and require no payment (some may need a free account for certificates):

Apollo Digital Free GEO Course — ~7 lessons / 100 minutes across foundations, technical readiness (crawlers, robots.txt), measurement (with free Google Sheets templates for audits and share-of-voice tracking), and tooling. No signup required; progress saves in-browser. Updated 2026.⁠Apollodigital
Ahrefs Academy AEO Course — 12 lessons / ~1 hour 26 minutes. Covers how AI search works, platform differences (ChatGPT, AI Overviews, Perplexity), content that gets cited, brand mentions, technical practices, and measurement. No signup required to watch; free Ahrefs certification exam available.⁠Ahrefs
HubSpot Academy AEO Fundamentals — Free certification course (~2 hours 20 minutes). Focuses on answer engines, metrics (visibility, share of voice, citations), content planning, and measurement. There’s also a related “SEO in the Age of AI” path.⁠Offers.hubspot
Other solid free options: Open University’s short (~1-hour) intro to GEO; Rank Prompt’s free ~90-minute GEO & AEO course; AISQ Growth’s GEO Academy; and Finseo Academy materials (video lessons, some with certificates).
Curated Learning Paths, Guides & Ebooks
LearningAIsearch.com (by Aleyda Solis) — Free, non-biased roadmap that organizes reliable free guides and resources across fundamentals, measurement, technical, content, and tools. Maintained by a respected international SEO/AI search consultant; also ties into the SEOFOMO newsletter.⁠Learningaisearch
Class Central: “25 Resources to Learn Generative Engine Optimization in 2026” — Aggregates free/paid courses, guides (e.g., iPullRank AI Search Manual), blogs (Neil Patel, Seer Interactive, Writesonic, Profound), YouTube channels (Nathan Gotch, Exposure Ninja, Ahrefs), and podcasts. Updated into 2026.⁠Classcentral
Stackmatix: Free AEO and GEO Learning Resources in 2026 — Practical overview of resource types (official docs, practitioner blogs, communities, research) plus a suggested multi-week learning path (fundamentals → competitive landscape → tactics → apply & measure).⁠Stackmatix
The GEO Lab free ebooks — Multiple free downloads (Pocket Guide 2026 Edition, SEO-to-GEO framework, experiments guide, 30-day workbook, technical WordPress guide, glossary, authority playbook, and full AI SEO OS). Based on their GEO Stack framework.⁠Thegeolab
Practitioner Blogs, Video, Communities & Tools
Blogs & newsletters: Neil Patel’s GEO content, Seer Interactive, Writesonic GEO archives, Profound, daydream, TripleDart, and Aleyda Solis’s SEOFOMO.
YouTube: Nathan Gotch, Ahrefs channel (AEO playlist), Exposure Ninja, SMA Marketing, and Finseo Academy playlists for short practical lessons.
Communities: Relevant Reddit communities, Discord/Slack groups focused on AI search (look for places sharing real citation data rather than pure theory).
Free measurement/tools: Google Search Console (AI impressions within Web search data), Bing Webmaster Tools (AI citations), free tiers or audits from various visibility checkers, plus spreadsheets for manual prompt tracking. Practice by running a fixed set of buyer prompts across engines before/after changes on a site you control.
Recommended Learning Path (2026)
Read the original GEO paper + Google’s official AI features docs for a clear baseline.
Complete one structured free course (Ahrefs AEO or Apollo Digital are strong starting points).
Use LearningAIsearch.com or the Class Central/Stackmatix roundups as hubs.
Apply tactics on a few pages, measure with free tools (prompt tracking + Search Console), and iterate.
Stay current via practitioner newsletters and communities—GEO evolves quickly as engines change.

Caveats: No single official “GEO certification” exists; certificates prove study more than results. The strongest signal is measurable before/after citation or mention improvements on prompts you control. Google’s stance is that core SEO still dominates for its AI features, while multi-engine strategies (content extractability, entity clarity, earned mentions, technical crawlability) matter more broadly. Quality varies—prioritize sources with real data or experiments over pure theory.⁠Pixis

These resources are free as of late 2026 and should give a solid, practical foundation without paid courses or tools.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Generative Engine Optimization (GEO) is the practice of optimizing content, entities, and technical signals so AI systems (ChatGPT, Perplexity, Google AI Overviews/AI Mode, Gemini, Claude, etc.) cite, mention, or recommend your brand/pages in synthesized answers rather than just ranking blue links. It overlaps heavily with Answer Engine Optimization (AEO) and traditional SEO fundamentals (crawlability, E-E-A-T, clear structure, authority).

Here are the strongest free resources available as of late 2026, prioritized for practical value from active practitioners and official sources.

Structured Free Courses (Best Starting Points)

These offer the fastest path from fundamentals to measurement:

Apollo Digital Free GEO Course — 7 lessons (~100 minutes) covering foundations, ChatGPT citations, technical readiness (robots.txt), measurement loops, and free Google Sheets templates (visibility audit + share-of-voice tracker). No signup required; progress saves in the browser. Runs entirely on free tools.⁠Apollodigital
Rank Prompt Free GEO & AEO Course — ~20 lessons (~90 minutes). Covers the GEO framework (internal vs external), on-site optimization (schema, crawlability, llms.txt context), off-site citations, and packaging GEO as a service. Free with templates/SOPs.⁠Rankprompt
Ahrefs Academy Answer Engine Optimization (AEO) Course — 12 lessons (1 hour 26 minutes). Explains how AI search works, platform differences (Google AI Overviews, ChatGPT, Perplexity), brand gap analysis, content that gets cited, technical practices, and measurement. No signup required.⁠Ahrefs
HubSpot Academy AEO Fundamentals — Free certification course (~2 hours 20 minutes, 19 videos). Covers how answer engines select content, E-E-A-T, content structure for citations, and measurement. Account required but free.⁠Academy.hubspot
AISQ Growth GEO Academy — Free standalone course (5 lessons + worksheets/templates) focused on getting recommended by ChatGPT, Perplexity, Claude, and Google AI Overviews. No paywall.⁠Aisq
Botric GEO Foundations — Free practical modules (several hours of content available, more rolling out) on GEO vs SEO, how LLMs/RAG work, citation gaps, and the four pillars of AI visibility.⁠Botric

Other short free options appear on platforms like Udemy (search “GEO” or “AEO GEO 2026”) and Open University-style intros listed on Class Central.

Curated Learning Paths and Aggregators
LearningAIsearch.com (by Aleyda Solis) — Free, non-biased roadmap aggregating reliable free guides across fundamentals, technical setup, content, brand authority/citations, measurement, prompt research, and e-commerce/agentic commerce. Continuously updated; no ads or paid placements.⁠Learningaisearch
The GEO Community — Practitioner blog with experiment-backed guides (crawlability, schema, entity strategy, log analysis) plus a structured /start learning path with role-based tracks (Strategist, Builder, Engineer). Strong emphasis on live experiments and documented results.⁠Thegeocommunity
Class Central’s “25 Resources to Learn Generative Engine Optimization in 2026” — Roundup of free courses, blogs, tools, podcasts, and YouTube channels, with beginner-friendly recommendations.⁠Classcentral
Stackmatix free AEO/GEO resources overview — Comparison of resource types plus a practical 4-phase learning path (fundamentals → competitive landscape → tactical frameworks → apply & measure).⁠Stackmatix
Foundational Research (Read These Early)
Original GEO paper: “GEO: Generative Engine Optimization” by Aggarwal et al. (Princeton/IIT Delhi, KDD 2024, arXiv:2311.09735). Defines the problem, tests strategies (e.g., statistics, expert quotes, fluency, citations) across thousands of queries, and reports substantial citation lifts. Primary source for the field.⁠Thegeocommunity
Follow-up comparative studies (e.g., across ChatGPT, Claude, Perplexity, Gemini) and related work such as AutoGEO frameworks.
Free Ebooks, Guides, and Practitioner Content
The GEO Lab free ebooks — Multiple titles including The GEO Pocket Guide (2026), SEO to GEO: The Complete Framework, GEO Experiments, workbook, WordPress technical guide, glossary, field manual, and authority playbook. All free (email optional via Gumroad; price $0). Built around a consistent GEO Stack framework.⁠Thegeolab
Neil Patel’s GEO guide and related posts.
iPullRank AI Search Manual (free blueprint).
Practitioner blogs from Seer Interactive, Profound, Goodie, Writesonic, and others for tactical depth and case data.
Official platform docs: Google Search Central material on AI features/Overviews and structured data; Bing Webmaster Tools AI visibility insights; crawler documentation from OpenAI, Perplexity, and Anthropic.
Free Tools for Practice and Measurement
Google Search Console (AI Overview/AI Mode impressions where available).
Bing Webmaster Tools (AI citation data).
Free tiers or limited views from tools like Ahrefs Brand Radar concepts, plus free audit helpers and Sheets templates from the courses above.
Manual testing: Run the same prompts across ChatGPT, Perplexity, and Google AI experiences and track mentions.
Recommended Learning Path (Self-Study)
Week 1: Read the original arXiv GEO paper + one short course (Ahrefs AEO or Apollo Digital) for foundations.
Weeks 2–3: Work through LearningAIsearch.com or The GEO Community /start path; complete HubSpot or Rank Prompt for structure and measurement.
Ongoing: Apply tactics (lead with clear, quotable answers; add statistics/sources; strengthen entity signals and crawlability) on 3–5 pages. Measure with free tools + manual prompt testing. Supplement with practitioner blogs and the GEO Lab pocket guide.
Track results and iterate—real citation data from your own site is the best feedback.

GEO evolves quickly with AI platform changes, so prioritize recent practitioner content and primary research over generic theory. Combine one structured course with the Aleyda Solis roadmap or GEO Community materials for the most efficient free path. Official Google/Bing documentation provides the authoritative baseline for their ecosystems.
