回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Credible experiments show that certain GEO tactics can increase a brand’s share of visibility or citations once a source is already retrieved into an AI model’s context, but there is little strong evidence of stable, cross-platform causal effects on organic discoverability (getting retrieved in the first place) or on brand mentions/recommendations at scale. A 2026 critical survey of 45 studies reached this conclusion.⁠Geoaura

Foundational Controlled Study: Princeton GEO Paper

The most-cited rigorous experiment is “GEO: Generative Engine Optimization” by Aggarwal et al. (Princeton, Georgia Tech, and others; arXiv:2311.09735, presented at KDD 2024).⁠Collaborate.princeton

Method: GEO-bench with ~10,000 real queries. Content modifications were applied to sources already placed in a generative engine’s context (primarily an open-source GPT-3.5-style system, with some validation on Perplexity). Visibility was measured mainly via position-adjusted word count (share of the answer text attributable to the source, weighted by position).
Results (relative lifts on the main metric, approximate):
Expert quotations: +41%
Statistics addition: +33%
Fluency optimization: +29%
Citing sources: +28%
Quotation addition: +21%
Keyword stuffing: −8% (harmful)
Lower-ranked sites saw larger relative gains (up to ~115% in some cases for fifth-ranked pages under certain tactics). The widely quoted “up to 40%” figure refers to this conditional share-of-answer improvement, not a guaranteed increase in mention/citation probability or traffic.
Key caveat: Effects are conditional on the source already being retrieved. Later analyses and replications note that some volume-controlled tests of the same tactics showed little or no pooled effect when relevance is controlled, and the gains are better understood as redistributive within fixed context rather than pure discovery lifts.⁠Geoaura
Controlled Tests of Specific Tactics (Mostly Null for Schema)
Ahrefs schema experiment (2026): Difference-in-differences analysis of 1,885 pages that added JSON-LD schema vs. ~4,000 matched controls. Adding schema produced no meaningful citation lift (Google AI Mode +2.4%, ChatGPT +2.2% — both within noise; Google AI Overviews −4.6%). Observational correlations (cited pages more often have schema) did not hold as causal effects for already-visible pages.⁠Ahrefs
Synthetic-brand RCT on schema (Bouet, 2026): Randomized controlled trial with eight invented low-authority running-shoe brands (four with Schema.org markup, four controls). On Gemini with Google Search grounding, schema brands showed essentially identical mention rates to controls (~8.8%). The study ruled out large industry-claimed gains and attributed prior correlations to confounding by authority. Low-authority pages were rarely retrieved, so markup never reached the model.⁠Rucforsk.ruc
Competitive / Factorial Experiments on Citation Preference

A 2026 controlled study (“What Gets Cited,” arXiv:2605.25517) ran 252,000 trials across six LLMs. It injected exactly two competing sources into context (differing in one factor at a time), with brand anonymization and order counterbalancing.⁠arXiv

Strongest drivers of being cited first: topical relevance and list position (near-deterministic).
Consistent helps: explicit price information and recent timestamps.
Smaller gains: completeness and trust cues.
Negligible: pure formatting edits.

This reinforces that content factors matter mainly in competition after retrieval.

Critical Survey Synthesis

Olivier Martinez’s 2026 critical survey (arXiv:2607.14035) reviewed 45 studies (2023–2026). It found solid evidence that tactics can change how an already-retrieved page is cited or used, but no reviewed technique showed a stable, longitudinal, cross-platform causal effect on organic discoverability or downstream behavior. Relevance and position are the most reproducible levers; generic heuristics transfer poorly and can erode under competition. The Princeton gains are valid in their fixed-context setting but do not establish broader discovery effects.⁠arXiv

Practical / Quasi-Experimental Work
Search Engine Land structured experiments (2026): Two before/after tests (existing brand over months; cold-start SaaS agency over 30 days) tracking commercial keywords across multiple platforms. Placing listicles and other content on sources already commonly cited by AI produced clear increases in appearances and citations (hundreds of citation events; cold-start yielded 298 appearances). Earned third-party placements outperformed owned content; concentration on a few sources was extreme. These are not randomized but are among the more transparent real-world measurements of off-site tactics.⁠Searchengineland
Large observational baselines (e.g., Kumar et al. arXiv:2606.20065 on 100k+ responses; Seer Interactive’s 231k+ response GEO Olympics study) document brand-stature ladders, the dominance of third-party/earned sources for recommendations, and signal architecture effects (entity authority + third-party validation + community discussion), but they are primarily correlational rather than interventional experiments.
Overall Takeaways from the Evidence
Tactic Category	Evidence Strength for Increasing Mentions/Citations	Notes
On-page evidence (stats, expert quotes, citations, fluency)	Moderate (conditional on retrieval)	Princeton and related; mainly share-of-answer once in context
Schema / structured data	Weak / null	Ahrefs DiD + synthetic RCT
Off-site placements / listicles on already-cited sources	Practical positive signals	Quasi-experiments; third-party mentions strongly correlated in large studies
Pure formatting or keyword stuffing	Weak or negative	Multiple studies

Bottom line: GEO tactics that make content more extractable, verifiable, and relevant can improve how a brand is used if the model already retrieves the material. Getting retrieved (and becoming a known entity that models mention or query for) appears driven more by existing authority, third-party mentions, and retrieval match than by isolated on-page “GEO tricks.” Rigorous causal evidence for large, generalizable lifts in brand recommendations remains limited.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Credible evidence on GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) tactics and AI brand mentions/recommendations is still limited and mixed. Controlled academic experiments show content-level changes can increase a source’s share of an already-generated answer, but large surveys and later work find little stable causal effect on organic discoverability (getting retrieved in the first place). Observational studies and practitioner experiments provide stronger signals for off-site mentions, listicles, and entity signals.

Foundational Controlled Experiment: Aggarwal et al. (KDD 2024)

The seminal peer-reviewed paper “GEO: Generative Engine Optimization” (Aggarwal, Murahari, Rajpurohit, Kalyan, Narasimhan, Deshpande; arXiv:2311.09735, published in Proceedings of KDD 2024) introduced GEO-bench (10,000 diverse queries).

They tested black-box content rewrites on sources already retrieved into a generative engine context (custom GPT-3.5 setup + real Perplexity.ai tests). Key results on position-adjusted word count (a metric weighting how much of the answer draws from a source, adjusted for position):

Quotation addition: ~+41% relative
Statistics addition: ~+33%
Citing sources: ~+28%
Fluency optimization: ~+29%
Keyword stuffing: negative (~–8%)

Overall gains reached up to ~40% relative visibility; lower-ranked sources gained the most (e.g., Cite Sources +115% for rank-5 sources). They also reported up to ~37% improvement on live Perplexity.ai.

Limitations: This measures how already-retrieved content is used/cited inside answers, not whether tactics increase retrieval or brand-name mentions in open real-world AI search. Domain effects vary.

Critical Survey of 45 Studies (Martinez, arXiv:2607.14035, July 2026)

A systematic review of 45 GEO studies (Nov 2023–July 2026) concludes:

Strong evidence that interventions alter citation, prominence, or absorption once content is already retrieved into context.
No technique shows a stable, cross-platform, longitudinal causal effect on organic discoverability (getting into the retrieved set).
Topical relevance and list/position in context are the most reproducible drivers.
Many popular heuristics (formatting, fluency, statistics, quotations) transfer poorly or become null/negative under competition or different engines.
End-to-end tests (e.g., SAGEO Arena) show some rewrites can even degrade upstream retrieval.

It highlights later work such as C-SEO Bench (most conversational optimization methods ineffective or harmful) and the 252k-trial study below.

Large-Scale Controlled Citation Experiment: Vishwakarma et al. (“What Gets Cited,” arXiv:2605.25517)

This factorial study ran 252,000 trials across six LLMs in a controlled two-document RAG setup (exactly two sources injected; brands anonymized; order counterbalanced).

Top drivers of being cited first: topical relevance and list position (large effects). Explicit price information and a recent timestamp also helped consistently. Completeness and trust cues gave smaller gains; pure formatting edits had little impact.

This directly tests competitive GEO factors relevant to recommendations but still within a fixed-context (already-retrieved) setting.

Large Observational Baseline: Kumar et al. (“GEO at Scale,” arXiv:2606.20065)

Analysis of 100k+ prompt responses across 100+ brands (March–May 2026). Key findings:

Clear brand-stature ladder on unbranded prompts: household names ~73%, mid-market ~44%, niche ~11%.
~78% of citations go to corporate sites (mostly third-party); ranked “best-of” listicles are the single most-cited page type (~21%).
Sentiment is highly unstable relative to mere mention.

Primarily measurement/baseline rather than a tactic-intervention experiment; it proposes future protocols for causal tests.

Practitioner Real-World Experiments (Search Engine Land, 2026)

Two structured experiments tracked commercial-intent keywords manually across multiple AI platforms:

Existing brand: Listicle placements, PR, guest posts, and on-site content produced appearances for 10–12 of 15 keywords, with peak keyword presence ~37%. Listicles drove the majority of citations.
Cold-start SaaS agency (zero baseline AI presence): 298 appearances in 30 days. Earned placements on sources already observed in AI answers (e.g., Indie Hackers citations rose 232%) drove most mentions; owned content contributed less. Comparative listicle updates produced large relative gains.

These directly measured brand appearances in recommendation-style answers and challenge some conventional advice by emphasizing observed citation sources over generic on-page tactics.

Strong Observational Correlations (Ahrefs, 2025)

Studies of ~75,000 brands (Domain Rating >40) found:

Branded web mentions correlated ~0.66 with AI Overview visibility (far stronger than backlinks ~0.22).
Later cross-platform work (ChatGPT, AI Mode, AI Overviews) found YouTube mentions the strongest single predictor (~0.74), followed by branded web mentions (0.66–0.71).
Top-quartile mention brands received ~10× more AI Overview mentions than the next quartile.

These are correlations, not causal experiments, but they consistently point to off-site entity/brand signals (mentions, YouTube, anchors) over traditional on-site SEO metrics.

Other Notable Work
Seer Interactive GEO Olympics Study (2026): 231k+ LLM responses over 52 days during the Olympics. Strong “signal architecture” (entity authority + third-party validation + community discussion) associated with ~7.8× more mentions. Citation sources differ sharply by query type (factual vs. judgment/recommendation) and platform.
Various OtterlyAI and agency case experiments test narrow tactics (e.g., publication-date updates, awards) with mixed, often modest results.
Bottom Line
What has been measured: Content rewrites (quotes, stats, citations) can increase a page’s share of an answer when it is already retrieved (Aggarwal et al.). Competitive factors like relevance and position dominate first-citation odds (Vishwakarma et al.). Real-world placements on listicles and earned mentions can produce measurable rises in brand appearances from a low base.
What remains weakly supported: Stable, cross-platform causal lifts in organic retrieval or durable recommendation share. Large reviews find no such consistent effect; gains are often redistributive or eroded by competition, and visibility is stochastic (high day-to-day variability).
Off-site brand mentions, YouTube presence, third-party listicles, and entity signals show the strongest observational associations with being named or recommended.

Sources: Aggarwal et al. (KDD 2024 / arXiv:2311.09735), Martinez (arXiv:2607.14035), Vishwakarma et al. (arXiv:2605.25517), Kumar et al. (arXiv:2606.20065), Ahrefs 75k-brand studies (2025), Search Engine Land experiments (2026), Seer Interactive GEO Olympics (2026).

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
The GEO Lab (Artur Ferreira) and practitioners like Sam Shev stand out for practical, data-backed GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) tutorials suited to independent builders.⁠Thegeolab

GEO focuses on getting content cited in AI answers from tools like ChatGPT, Perplexity, Google AI Overviews, and similar systems, rather than traditional search rankings.

The GEO Lab (thegeolab.net)

Artur Ferreira’s GEO Lab publishes a free library of practitioner resources, including the GEO Field Manual (a comprehensive working guide), Pocket Guide, GEO Experiments, 30-day Workbook, and related materials. These center on the five-layer GEO Stack (Retrieval Probability, Extractability, Entity Reinforcement, Structural Authority, and System Memory).⁠Thegeolab

They emphasize:

Diagnostic audits and section-level scoring.
Experiment templates and measurement (including their own tests, e.g., declarative structures outperforming narrative ones in citation rates).
Practical implementation: answer-first openings, extractable chunks, schema, entity consistency, and 30-day starter roadmaps.
References to primary research, including the foundational Princeton/IIT Delhi KDD 2024 paper.

These are free for personal and commercial use, structured as reference tools rather than pure theory, and suitable for site owners or solo builders who want measurable, experiment-driven work without agency budgets.

Sam Shev and similar roadmaps

Sam Shev (fractional CMO focused on early-stage SaaS) published a detailed 90-Day Generative Engine Optimization Roadmap. It is explicitly grounded in the Aggarwal et al. (KDD 2024 / arXiv:2311.09735) study that tested content techniques across ~10,000 queries and found lifts from adding quotations (~+40%), statistics, fluency/clarity, and inline citations, with larger relative gains often going to lower-ranked or smaller sites. Keyword stuffing performed worse than baseline.⁠Samshev

The roadmap breaks into phases:

Baseline prompt audits (25–50 buyer questions across AI tools).
Technical fixes (crawler access, schema).
Rewrites of existing high-value pages using evidence-based techniques (Answer Capsule style, named quotes, stats with sources).
Content production, off-site signals, and KPI tracking (citation rate, AI share of voice, referral traffic).

It prioritizes maximizing existing content equity and repeatable processes, which fits independent or lean teams.

Other practical options
Apollo Digital offers a free ~100-minute GEO course with lessons on foundations, crawler access, measurement (including free Google Sheets audit templates), and tooling.
Julian Goldie and similar creators share free step-by-step systems using tools like Search Console exports plus free AI agents.
Indie-oriented pieces appear on sites like Indietool, HeyCatch, Solo Business Hub, and Indie Hackers, often covering 30-day plans, entity consistency, and low-cost distribution for solo founders.
Why these work for independent builders

The academic foundation (Aggarwal et al., KDD 2024) showed that substance—quotes, statistics, and citations—improves visibility inside AI answers more reliably than classic keyword tactics, and relative gains can be larger for smaller or mid-ranked sites. The best tutorials translate this into low-resource actions: manual prompt baselines, answer-first rewrites, schema markup, extractable structure, entity consistency across the web, and simple before/after measurement. They avoid heavy tool dependency at the start and focus on what independent builders can ship themselves.⁠Aisearch

Start with the GEO Lab Field Manual or Pocket Guide for a full framework, or Sam Shev’s roadmap for a concrete 90-day sequence. Always run your own baseline prompt tests, as AI citation behavior evolves. Primary source for the key numbers remains the 2024 KDD paper (arXiv:2311.09735).

5GEO: The Complete 21-Strategy Playbook (2026) | Solo Business Hub (Solobusinesshub)
https://www.solobusinesshub.com/launch-and-grow/geo/		www.solobusinesshub.cominline_citation
回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Several independent creators, tools, and practitioner communities publish the most practical, data-backed GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) tutorials aimed at independent/solo builders. GEO is the practice of structuring content and signals so generative AI systems (ChatGPT, Perplexity, Google AI Overviews/Gemini, Claude, Copilot, etc.) cite or recommend your pages/brand in answers rather than just ranking links.⁠Aisearch

Leading sources for independents

The GEO Community (thegeocommunity.com) stands out for rigorous, experiment-oriented material. It maintains a practical library of frameworks, implementation notes, and a blog covering crawlability (robots.txt for AI bots), schema, LLMs.txt, entity strategy, IndexNow tests, and evaluation. It offers a guided /start learning path with role-based tracks (including Builder) that sequence from foundational research to hands-on work. Content draws heavily on controlled experiments and the core academic paper rather than pure theory or sales pitches. Free resources and practitioner experiments make it well-suited to independents who want to test and measure themselves.⁠Thegeocommunity

HeyCatch (Vladyslava Sirychenko) published a dedicated July 2026 guide, “Generative Engine Optimization for Solo Founders.” It targets pre-traction builders with no audience, backlinks, or budget. The six-step framework covers on-site structure (semantic HTML + SoftwareApplication schema + 40–60-word extractable blocks), narrow topical content architecture (3–5 pieces with cited data), cross-source consistency (identical descriptions across site, Product Hunt, GitHub, Reddit, Indie Hackers, directories), extractable answer blocks under question headings, technical trust signals, and weekly monitoring. It emphasizes free tools (Rich Results Test, Search Console, curl) and sequential implementation in days, with a concrete 30-day example for a simple product. It cites statistics on zero-click search, AI Overview click impact, and evidence-based citation lifts.⁠Heycatch

IndieTool offers “GEO AI Search Optimization: A Practical Guide for 2026,” focused on one-product indie founders. It prioritizes entity signals (Organization/Product JSON-LD, consistent identity, author pages, independent mentions) over high-volume content, argues for distribution breadth (directories, comparisons, community answers), and includes a clear 30-day plan (entity foundation → technical access → distribution → evaluation). It cites AI search visit growth and the Princeton study’s ~30–40% visibility lifts from quotations, statistics, and source citations.⁠Indietool

Solo Business Hub publishes the “GEO: The Complete 21-Strategy Playbook (2026),” explicitly labeled an actionable guide for solo builders. It organizes tactics into four layers (content quality with sources/stats/quotes; structure & extractability via chunking and answer blocks; distribution & retrieval; system & scale with templates and clusters). Advice is low-resource and template-friendly, with pro tips for solo workflows.⁠Solobusinesshub

Indie Hackers hosts multiple practical posts from independent founders: workflows for claim-level authority and pain-focused content, diagnoses of failed GEO experiments (often tracing back to indexing or crawler access), results from scanning thousands of sites (common issues: blocked AI bots, missing schema, no llms.txt), and AI-ready website field guides. These are experience-driven and low-overhead rather than systematically benchmarked.

Why these sources fit independent builders

GEO is still early. Most commercial guides and agencies target larger teams or enterprises with budgets for tools, content teams, and digital PR. Independent-focused publishers fill the gap with DIY, zero-to-low-budget advice that matches solo constraints: limited time, no team, need for compounding visibility without paid distribution.

They consistently lean on the same core data: the 2024 KDD paper “GEO: Generative Engine Optimization” by Aggarwal et al. (Princeton/IIT Delhi/Georgia Tech collaboration; arXiv:2311.09735). That study tested nine content tactics across a 10,000-query benchmark (GEO-bench) and found that adding quotations, statistics, and source citations produced the largest visibility gains (roughly 30–40% on position-adjusted word count metrics), while keyword stuffing underperformed the baseline. Relative gains were often largest for lower-ranked or smaller sites—directly relevant to independents. Secondary analyses and some live checks (e.g., on Perplexity) have reinforced the directional findings around evidence density and extractability, though the original metric measures share of generated answer text rather than traffic or clicks.⁠Aisearch

Practical tutorials therefore emphasize:

Answer-first / extractable blocks (40–60 words under question headings).
Schema (FAQPage, Article, Organization/Product, etc.) and crawl access for AI bots.
Consistent entity language across owned and third-party surfaces.
Original or well-cited data points and short attributed quotes.
Simple measurement via fixed prompt sets run manually across engines, plus Search Console.
Narrow topical depth and templates over scale.

These tactics are implementable with free tools and existing skills, and the academic result that evidence-rich structured content helps smaller sites disproportionately makes them high-leverage for independents.

Other general 90-day roadmaps (e.g., from various fractional CMOs or agencies) exist and often cite the same Princeton data, but they are less specifically calibrated to solo/pre-traction constraints. For the most relevant material, start with the GEO Community’s learning path and resources page, the HeyCatch solo-founder guide, the IndieTool 30-day plan, and the Solo Business Hub playbook, while reading the original Aggarwal et al. paper for the underlying evidence.

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Best free resources to learn GEO in 2026

Generative Engine Optimization (GEO) is the practice of improving the likelihood that AI-powered search and answer systems—such as ChatGPT, Google AI Overviews, Perplexity, Gemini, and Microsoft Copilot—discover, mention, cite, or recommend your content.

My recommendation is to combine one structured course, official platform documentation, and hands-on experiments. GEO is evolving quickly, and some widely promoted tactics have limited evidence behind them. Learning how to measure results is as important as learning optimization techniques.

1. Best resources to start with

1. Google Search Central — AI Optimization Guide

Start here

Free · Official documentation · Best for fundamentals

Google's official guide to optimizing for AI Overviews and AI Mode. It explains why traditional SEO remains foundational, why original and useful content matters, and which popular GEO tactics Google considers unnecessary.

Important takeaway: Google's guidance says there is no special schema markup or mandatory llms.txt file required for AI search visibility. 
Google for Developers
+1

Read the official guide

2. Ahrefs Academy — Answer Engine Optimization (AEO) Course

Free · 12 lessons · 1 hour 26 minutes · No sign-up required

My top recommendation for a structured introduction. The course covers how AI search works, differences between platforms, prompt research, content optimization, brand mentions, technical SEO, and measurement.

Although the course uses the term AEO, its lessons substantially overlap with GEO. 
Ahrefs
+1

Take the course

3. Learning AI Search — Aleyda Solis

Free · Curated learning roadmap · Updated March 2026

Best as a reference library after the introductory course. It organizes resources by fundamentals, content optimization, technical configuration, brand authority, citations, KPIs, and tools. It also links to platform documentation and practitioner guides.

This is a curated collection of resources rather than a single linear course. 
LearningAiSearch
+1

Explore the roadmap

4. HubSpot Academy — SEO in the Age of AI

Free · 6 lessons · 31 videos · 1 hour 51 minutes

A good alternative if you want a marketing-oriented curriculum. It covers platform-specific optimization for Google, ChatGPT, Perplexity, and Gemini, as well as video visibility and a 90-day implementation plan. A free account is required. 
HubSpot Academy

Take the course

5. Apollo Digital — Learn GEO

Free · 7 lessons · Approximately 100 minutes · No registration

Best for practical exercises. It includes a DIY AI visibility audit, a share-of-voice tracker, and free Google Sheets templates. The course was updated in August 2026. Because it is produced by an SEO agency, cross-check its recommendations against platform documentation. 
Apollo Digital

Start the course

6. Bing Webmaster Tools — AI Performance

Free · Official measurement documentation

Particularly useful if you own a website. The AI Performance report tracks pages cited in AI-generated answers, citation trends, and grounding queries across supported Microsoft Copilot and Bing experiences. You will need to verify your website to access its reporting data. 
Bing Webmaster Tools

Read the documentation

7. OpenAI — Overview of OpenAI Crawlers

Free · Official technical documentation

Read this when you need to understand how websites become discoverable through ChatGPT Search. It explains the distinction between OAI-SearchBot, which supports search visibility, and GPTBot, which relates to model training. Their robots.txt settings can be managed independently. 
OpenAI Developers

Read the crawler documentation

2. Research papers worth reading

These are especially useful if you want to understand the evidence behind GEO rather than simply follow industry advice.

8. Aggarwal et al. — GEO: Generative Engine Optimization (KDD 2024)

Free academic paper · Foundational research

This paper introduced the GEO framework and the GEO-bench benchmark. The authors reported visibility improvements of up to 40% under their experimental conditions, with results varying by domain. Treat that figure as a research finding, not a forecast for real-world traffic or a guaranteed outcome. 
arXiv

Read the paper on arXiv

9. Martinez — Optimizing Visibility in Generative Engines: A Critical Survey of GEO (2023–2026)

Free academic preprint · Published July 2026

A useful counterbalance to practitioner courses. The survey reviews 45 studies and examines retrieval, citation, measurement, and evidence quality. It highlights substantial variation across systems and finds that existing research does not establish stable, long-term, cross-platform causal effects from GEO techniques. 
arXiv

Read the survey on arXiv

4. A practical two-week learning plan

[Personal context omitted.] I would prioritize content experiments and measurement before investing time in technical website optimization.

Days 1–3

Learn the fundamentals

Complete the Ahrefs course and read Google's guide. Write down three hypotheses about what makes an AI system more likely to mention or cite a source.

Days 4–6

Establish a baseline

Select one topic you want to be known for. Create 10 realistic questions that your target audience might ask AI systems. Test them in ChatGPT Search, Perplexity, and Google's AI search features where available.

Record whether your content or account is mentioned, which sources are cited, and which competing sources appear.

# Best free resources to learn GEO in 2026

Generative Engine Optimization (GEO) focuses on improving how brands, websites, and creators appear in AI-generated answers, citations, and recommendations across platforms such as ChatGPT, Google AI Overviews, Gemini, and Perplexity.

**My recommendation is to learn GEO through three complementary approaches\:** official documentation to understand how AI search works, research papers to separate evidence from marketing claims, and hands-on experiments to test which tactics work for your own content.

One important caveat: a July 2026 research survey reviewing 45 GEO studies found that evidence for reliable, long-term improvements in organic discoverability across different AI platforms remains limited. Treat GEO as an experimental discipline, and measure results rather than assuming a particular tactic works. citeturn805664academia27

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
GEO 策略究竟能否提高 AI 品牌提及率？实验研究综述

截至 2026 年 10 月，已有学术论文、大规模 AI 搜索数据分析和实际品牌优化实验研究 GEO（Generative Engine Optimization）的效果。证据支持几个方向，但不同研究的可信度和结论强度存在明显差异。

核心结论：部分 GEO 策略确实能提高内容在生成式搜索结果中的可见度；第三方媒体曝光、比较型内容和品牌在多个来源中的出现，也与 AI 品牌推荐存在关联。不过，能在真实 AI 搜索环境中，通过严格对照实验证明某项策略稳定提高品牌提及率或推荐排名的研究仍然有限。

尤其需要区分三个指标：

品牌提及（Brand Mentions）：AI 的回答里有没有出现品牌名称。

来源引用（Citations）：AI 有没有引用品牌官网或第三方网页。

推荐排名（Recommendation Rank）：品牌有没有进入推荐列表，以及排在第几位。

这三个指标相关，但不能相互替代。下面按证据强度梳理值得关注的研究。

1. Controlled experiments: the strongest evidence
1. GEO: Generative Engine Optimization (2024)
Peer-reviewed
Controlled benchmark

Pranjal Aggarwal et al. · ACM SIGKDD 2024

Read the paper · ACM DOI

This is the foundational GEO experiment. The researchers developed GEO-bench, containing 10,000 queries, and systematically modified source content to measure how those modifications affected its visibility in generated answers. Their main benchmark used Google-retrieved sources and GPT-3.5 to generate responses. 
arXiv
+1

Results

Content modification

	

Reported result




Add citations to reliable sources

	

Among the strongest strategies




Add credible quotations

	

Among the strongest strategies




Add relevant statistics

	

Among the strongest strategies




Improve fluency and readability

	

Also improved visibility




Keyword stuffing

	

Generally ineffective; 10% worse than baseline on the reported Perplexity visibility metric

The leading strategies improved Position-Adjusted Word Count by approximately 30–40% and Subjective Impression by 15–30% in the main benchmark. These are measures of a source's prominence and influence in an answer, not percentages of additional brand recommendations. 
arXiv
+1

The researchers also tested Perplexity. Quotation additions improved their position-adjusted metric by 22%; the reported gains for statistics and citations reached 37% on the subjective metric. An important limitation: because they could not specify source URLs directly, they uploaded source text to Perplexity. This tested how the model used supplied content, rather than whether publishing a revised webpage would cause organic AI search to retrieve it. 
arXiv
+1

Assessment: Strong evidence that content changes can affect source visibility under controlled conditions. Limited evidence for increased brand mentions or recommendations in live AI search.

2. C-SEO Bench: Does Conversational SEO Work? (2025)
Peer-reviewed

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
As of October 2026, there is credible evidence that GEO tactics can improve AI-generated search visibility, but much weaker evidence that they reliably increase a brand’s organic mentions or recommendations across AI platforms.

The most useful research separates three outcomes:

Citation visibility: Does the AI cite your website or content?

Brand mentions: Does the AI actually name your brand in its answer?

Recommendations: Does the AI recommend your brand over competitors?

These outcomes can move independently. Several experiments have increased citations without producing a measurable increase in brand mentions.

1. The most credible experiments
Strongest controlled evidence

1. Aggarwal et al. — GEO: Generative Engine Optimization (KDD 2024)

Academic paper · Peer-reviewed conference publication

回答正文 · 公开脱敏副本
Practical, data-backed GEO resources for independent builders

For independent builders, I would prioritize publishers that combine repeatable experiments, step-by-step implementation guides, and measurable outcomes. GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) aims to improve how often AI-powered search and answer engines mention, recommend, or cite your brand and content.

The most useful resources fall into three groups:

Practical tutorials: how to change content, publish evidence, and improve discoverability.

Data-driven research: what actually changes AI mentions and citations, supported by experiments or observational data.

Measurement frameworks: how to determine whether your work produces a real improvement.

One important caveat: GEO is still an emerging field. A tactic that improves citations in one experiment may not work across ChatGPT, Perplexity, Gemini, and Google AI Overviews. The original GEO research introduced measurable content-optimization techniques, while later research continues to examine their limitations and generalizability. 【turn522626search7】【turn522626academia30】

阅读边界
记录反映测试当时账号、地区、界面模式与可见搜索状态下的回答。新会话不等于独立账号，也不保证平台记忆或个性化关闭。只有界面模式标签时，不能据此确认底层模型版本。
两次重复的样本量很小。推荐和引用的变化可能来自检索结果、生成随机性、索引更新及账号上下文。回答中的实验结论与推荐理由保留为平台原文，不等于本报告已独立复核其真实性。未判定值不计为“否”；引用映射完整性与正文状态分别记录。
本报告由已采集原文和逐项观察记录生成，可离线阅读。外部研究来源由各站点提供；本页不包含私有会话、原始截图或本地证据档案。所有汇总均可由原始记录复算。
